Exact dg categories I: foundations
Xiaofa Chen

Abstract
. We introduce the notion of exact dg category, which provides a differential graded enhancement of Nakaoka–Palu’s notion of extriangulated category. We give a definition in complete analogy with Quillen’s but where the category of kernel-cokernel pairs is replaced with a more sophisticated homotopy category. We introduce the notion of stable dg category, and prove that the H 0 H^{0} -category of an exact dg category 𝒜 \mathcal{A} is triangulated if and only if 𝒜 \mathcal{A} is stable. We illustrate our theory with several examples including the homotopy category of two-term complexes and Amiot’s fundamental domain for generalized cluster categories.



2020 Mathematics Subject Classification
18G35, 18G80, 18N40, 16G10,16E45

1.
Introduction

In this paper, we introduce and study the notion of
exact dg category
.
Let us illustrate the position of this class of categories among the other types of
categories that are relevant in the following diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\text{pretri. dg cat.}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\text{stable $\infty$-cat.}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\text{tri. cat.}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\text{Quillen ex. cat.}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\text{ex. dg cat.}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\text{Barwick ex. $\infty$-cat.}\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{\text{extri. cat.}\}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{dg}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{dg}}

Here, the black arrows denote inclusions of classes and the red arrows send a dg category (respectively an

\infty
-category)
\mathcal{A}
to
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
(respectively
h\mathcal{A}
). The dg nerve functor
N_{dg}

[
32
, Construction 1.3.1.6]
sends a dg category to an
\infty
-category. As we see in the diagram, the notion of exact dg category is

1)

a dg version of Barwick’s
[
2
]
notion of exact
\infty
-category;

2)

a simultaneous generalization of the notions of exact category in the sense of Quillen
[
38
]
and of
pretriangulated dg category in the sense of Bondal–Kapranov
[
4
]
, and

3)

a dg enhancement of the notion of extriangulated category in the sense of Nakaoka–Palu

[
35
]
in analogy with Bondal–Kapranov’s dg enhancement of the notion of triangulated category.

Let us emphasize that our notion of exact dg category is completely different from Positselski’s notion introduced in
[
37
]
: an exact structure in our sense can be transported along a quasi-equivalence, cf. Remark
4.5
b), which is not the case for an exact structure in Positselski’s sense.

The notion of an extriangulated category was introduced by Nakaoka–Palu in
[
35
]
.
It is a simultaneous generalization of Quillen’s notion of exact category
[
38
]
and
Grothendieck–Verdier’s notion of a triangulated category
[
43
]
.
Their aim was to give a convenient setup for writing down proofs
which apply to both exact categories and triangulated categories, and more generally to
extension-closed subcategories of triangulated categories.
The theory of extriangulated categories has developed a lot since their introduction in 2019
and many notions and constructions have been generalized to this setting (or the more general
setting of
n
-exangulated categories
[
16
]
).
The notion of extriangulated category plays an important role in the additive categorification of cluster algebras with coefficients, see for example in
[
45
,
28
,
44
,
12
]
.
Our aim in this work is to introduce the notion of exact dg category. It enhances Nakaoka–Palu’s notion of extriangulated category, cf. Theorem
A
.

Let us start by introducing the notion of
homotopy short exact sequence
. It is the key ingredient in the definition of an exact dg category.
Let
\mathcal{A}
be an
additive
dg category, i.e. the category
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
is additive.
The category
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})
of
3-term homotopy complexes over
\mathcal{A}
is the
H^{0}
-category of the dg category of strictly unital
{A}_{\infty}
-functors
from a certain index category to the dg category
\mathcal{A}
, cf. Definition
3.14
.
Its objects are identified with 3-term h-complexes, i.e. diagrams in
\mathcal{A}

(1.0.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

where
|f|=|j|=0
,
|h|=-1
and
d(f)=0
,
d(j)=0
and
d(h)=-jf
.
For a morphism
m:A\rightarrow B
in
\mathcal{A}
, we denote by
m^{\wedge}:A^{\wedge}\rightarrow B^{\wedge}
its image under the Yoneda dg functor. A 3-term h-complex over
\mathcal{A}
(
1.0.1
) yields a canonical morphism in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})

u:A_{0}^{\wedge}\rightarrow\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{Cone}(j^{\wedge}:A_{1}^{\wedge}\rightarrow A_{2}^{\wedge}),

where
u=\begin{bmatrix}f^{\wedge}\\
h^{\wedge}\end{bmatrix}
.

Definition

(Lemma
3.20
)
.

A
3
-term h-complex (
1.0.1
) is homotopy left exact if the map
\tau_{\leq 0}(u)
is a quasi-isomorphism of dg
\tau_{\leq 0}(\mathcal{A})
-modules. Dually, we define the notion of homotopy right exact sequence and then the notion of homotopy short exact sequence.

Using the above terminology, we define an
exact structure
on an additive dg category
\mathcal{A}

to be a class
\mathcal{S}
of homotopy short exact sequences in
\mathcal{A}
, satisfying certain axioms analogous to those of Quillen. In this case, we call the pair
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{S})
, or simply
\mathcal{A}
, an
exact dg category
.
Roughly speaking, an exact dg category is a homotopical version of Quillen’s exact category in the context of dg categories.
We refer to Section
4
for more details.

Our first result states that exact dg categories do enhance extriangulated categories. More precisely, we have the following theorem.

Theorem A

(Theorem
4.25
)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an exact dg category. Then
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
has a canonical extriangulated structure.

Extriangulated categories of this form are called
algebraic
. An analogous result for exact
\infty
-categories is due to Nakaoka–Palu
[
34
]
.
Since the notion of exact structure on an additive dg category
\mathcal{A}
is defined in terms of homotopy short exact sequences, which depends only on the connective cover
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}
of
\mathcal{A}
, the inclusion
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}
induces a bijection between the exact structures on both sides.
Consequently, every algebraic extriangulated category possesses a connective dg enhancement.
It is worth noting that an extension-closed full dg subcategory of an exact dg category inherits a canonical exact structure. As a result, both Yilin Wu’s Higgs categories
[
45
]
and Haibo Jin’s categories of Cohen–Macaulay dg modules
[
19
]
fall into the framework of algebraic extriangulated categories.

The following new notion is a dg analog of Lurie’s notion of stable
\infty
-category
[
32
]
.

Definition

(Definition
6.1
)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an additive dg category.
It is stable if the following conditions are satisfied:

(a)

the dg category
\mathcal{A}
admits homotopy kernels and homotopy cokernels;

(b)

a 3-term homotopy complex is homotopy left exact if and only if it is homotopy right exact.

We will show in a subsequent paper that a dg category
\mathcal{A}
is stable if and only if
N_{dg}(\mathcal{A})
is stable as an
\infty
-category. A pretriangulated dg category is necessarily stable. But the converse is not true, e.g. the
\tau_{\leq 0}
-truncation of a pretriangulated dg category is stable, but never pretriangulated. We refer to Section
6
for more details.
It turns out that a stable dg category has a canonical exact structure, cf. Proposition
6.4
.
Based on this, we can characterise when the extriangulated structure in Theorem
A
is triangulated.

Theorem B

(Theorem
6.5
)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a small exact dg category.
Then the extriangulated structure on
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
of Theorem
A
is a triangulated structure if and only if
\mathcal{A}
is a stable dg category with the canonical exact structure.

In a subsequent paper, we will study in detail the relations between exact dg categories and Barwick exact
\infty
-categories, characterize the class of algebraic extriangulated categories, and prove that each connective exact dg category embeds into its bounded dg derived category.
In particular, we will show that a connective stable dg category is quasi-equivalent to the
\tau_{\leq 0}
-truncation of a pretriangulated dg category.

The paper is structured as follows. In Section
2
, we collect basic notations and terminology about dg categories,
{A}_{\infty}
-categories and homotopy diagrams. In Section
3
, we introduce the notion of homotopy short exact sequence in a dg category. We then prove several useful diagram lemmas.
In Section
4
, we introduce the central notion in this paper: that of exact dg category. We show that for a small exact dg category
\mathcal{A}
, there exists a canonical extriangulated structure
(H^{0}(\mathcal{A}),\mathbb{E},\mathfrak{s})
on
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
.
In Section
5
, we illustrate our theory by the example of dg categories of two-term complexes of finitely generated projective modules over a ring.
In Section
6
, we study a special class of additive dg categories: the class of stable dg categories. We show that each stable dg category carries a canonical exact structure whose associated extriangulated structure is triangulated.

Throughout the paper, we assume that all the categories considered are small.

2.
Recollections on dg categories,
{A}_{\infty}
-categories and homotopy diagrams

In this section, we collect basic notations and terminology needed in this paper.

2.1.
Dg categories

Throughout we fix a commutative ring
k
. The standard references for dg categories are
[
21
,
25
,
8
,
42
,
4
]
.

We write
\otimes
for the tensor product over
k
.
We denote by
\mathcal{C}_{dg}(k)
the dg category of complexes of
k
-modules and by
\mathcal{C}(k)
the category of complexes of
k
-modules.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a dg
k
-category.
For two objects
A_{1}
and
A_{2}
, the Hom complex is denoted by
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(A_{1},A_{2})
or

\mathcal{A}(A_{1},A_{2})
. An element
f
of
\mathcal{A}(A_{1},A_{2})^{p}
will be called a
homogeneous
morphism of degree
p
with the notation
|f|=p
.
A homogeneous morphism
f:A_{1}\rightarrow A_{2}
is
closed
if we have
d(f)=0
.

Let
M
be complex of
k
-modules. We put

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\leq 0}M=(\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{-2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M^{-1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z^{0}M}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots).}

For a dg category
\mathcal{A}
, denote by
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}
the dg category with the same objects as
\mathcal{A}

and whose morphism complexes are given by

(\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A})(A_{1},A_{2})=\tau_{\leq 0}(\mathcal{A}(A_{1},A_{2})).

The composition is naturally induced by that of
\mathcal{A}
.

A dg category
\mathcal{A}
is
connective
if for each pair of objects
A_{1},A_{2}\in\mathcal{A}
, the
complex
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(A_{1},A_{2})
has cohomology concentrated in non-positive degrees.
It is called
strictly connective
if the components of the complex
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(A_{1},A_{2})
vanish
in all positive degrees.
Following Toën, the dg category
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}
is called the
connective cover of
\mathcal{A}
.

We denote by
Z^{0}(\mathcal{A})
the category with the same objects as
\mathcal{A}
and whose morphism
spaces are defined by

(Z^{0}\mathcal{A})(A_{1},A_{2})=Z^{0}(\mathcal{A}(A_{1},A_{2})),

where
Z^{0}
is the kernel of
d:\mathcal{A}(A_{1},A_{2})^{0}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}(A_{1},A_{2})^{1}
.
Similarly, we denote by
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
the category with the same objects as
\mathcal{A}
and whose
morphism spaces are given by

(H^{0}\mathcal{A})(A_{1},A_{2})=H^{0}(\mathcal{A}(A_{1},A_{2})),

where
H^{0}
denotes the zeroth cohomology of the complex.

The
oppositie dg category

\mathcal{A}^{op}
has the same objects as
\mathcal{A}
and its morphism spaces are defined by

\mathcal{A}^{op}(X,Y)=\mathcal{A}(Y,X);

the composition of
f\in\mathcal{A}^{op}(Y,X)^{p}
with
g\in\mathcal{A}^{op}(Z,Y)^{q}
is given by
(-1)^{pq}gf
.
By a dg
\mathcal{A}
-module
M
, we mean a right dg
\mathcal{A}
-module, i.e. a dg functor
\mathcal{A}^{op}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{dg}(k)
.
We denote by
\mathcal{C}_{dg}(\mathcal{A})
the dg category of right dg
\mathcal{A}
-modules. The category of dg

\mathcal{A}
-modules is

\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})=Z^{0}(\mathcal{C}_{dg}(\mathcal{A})).

The category up to homotopy of dg
\mathcal{A}
-modules is

\mathcal{H}(\mathcal{A})=H^{0}(\mathcal{C}_{dg}(\mathcal{A})).

An object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
is
contractible
if it is a zero object in
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})
, or equivalently if we have
\mathrm{id}_{A}=d(h)
for some morphism
h:A\rightarrow A
of degree
-1
.
A dg
\mathcal{A}
-module is
contractible
if it is contractible in the dg category
\mathcal{C}_{dg}(\mathcal{A})
.

For each object
A\in\mathcal{A}
, we have the right dg module
represented by

A

A^{\wedge}=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(-,A).

A dg
\mathcal{A}
-module
M
is
representable
if it is isomorphic to
A^{\wedge}
for some

A\in\mathcal{A}
and
quasi-representable
if it is quasi-isomorphic to
A^{\wedge}
for some
A\in\mathcal{A}
.

For a dg
\mathcal{A}
-module
M
, put
IM=\mathrm{Cone}(\mathrm{id}_{M})
and
PM=\mathrm{Cone}(\mathrm{id}_{\Sigma^{-1}M})
.
Explicitly, we have
IM=\Sigma M\oplus M
and
PM=M\oplus\Sigma^{-1}M
as graded
\mathcal{A}
-modules.
We have the natural inclusion
i=[0,1]^{\intercal}:M\rightarrow IM
and projection
p=[1,0]:PM\rightarrow M
.

A dg functor
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
is a
quasi-equivalence
if

a)

it is
quasi-fully faithful
, i.e. for all objects
A,A^{\prime}\in A
, the morphism

F_{A,A^{\prime}}:\mathcal{A}(A,A^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(FA,FA^{\prime}).

is a quasi-isomorphism, and

b)

the induced functor
H^{0}(F):H^{0}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{B})
is an equivalence of categories.

The
Yoneda dg functor

\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{dg}(\mathcal{A}),\;\;A\mapsto A^{\wedge}

is fully faithful.
Let
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
be the full dg subcategory of
\mathcal{C}_{dg}(\mathcal{A})
consisting of objects
A^{\wedge}
,
\mathrm{Cone}(\mathrm{id}_{A^{\wedge}})
and
\mathrm{Cone}(-\mathrm{id}_{\Sigma^{-1}A^{\wedge}})
for each
A\in\mathcal{A}
.
The dg category
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
has contractible pre-covers and contractible pre-envelopes and the inclusion
\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
is a quasi-equivalence.
Thus, up to quasi-equivalence, we may assume
\mathcal{A}
has contractible pre-covers and contractible pre-envelopes.

The category
\mathrm{dgcat}
of small dg categories admits the Dwyer-Kan model structure (cf.
[
39
]
),
whose weak equivalences are the quasi-equivalences. Its homotopy category is denoted by
\mathrm{Hqe}
.
There exists a cofibrant replacement functor
Q
on
\mathrm{dgcat}
such that for any
\mathcal{A}\in\mathrm{dgcat}
, the natural dg functor
Q(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{A}
is the identity on the set of objects.
In particular, the Hom-complexes of
Q(\mathcal{A})
are cofibrant over
k
.
The
tensor product

\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B}
of two dg categories
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
has the class of objects
\mathrm{obj}(\mathcal{A})\times\mathrm{obj}(\mathcal{B})
and the morphism spaces

(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B})((A,B),(A^{\prime},B^{\prime}))=\mathcal{A}(A,A^{\prime})\otimes\mathcal{B}(B,B^{\prime})

with the natural compositions and units.
This defines a symmetric monoidal structure
-\otimes-
on
\mathrm{dgcat}
which is closed.
For
\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B}\in\mathrm{dgcat}
, put
\mathcal{A}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{B}=\mathcal{A}\otimes Q(\mathcal{B})
.
This extends to a bifunctor
-\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}-:\mathrm{dgcat}\times\mathrm{dgcat}\rightarrow\mathrm{dgcat}
and then passes through the homotopy categories

-\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}-:\mathrm{Hqe}\times\mathrm{Hqe}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hqe}.

Let
\mathrm{Cat}
be the category of small categories.
Let
[\mathrm{Cat}]
be the category with objects small categories and with morphisms isomorphism classes of functors.
In particular the isomorphism class of an equivalence of categories is an isomorphism in
[\mathrm{Cat}]
.
So the functor
H^{0}:\mathrm{dgcat}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
induces a functor

H^{0}:\mathrm{Hqe}\rightarrow[\mathrm{Cat}].

For a category
\mathcal{C}
, we denote by
\mathrm{Iso}(\mathcal{C})
the class of its isomorphism classes of objects.

For a dg category
\mathcal{A}
, we denote by
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
its
derived category
, a triangulated category.
By definition,
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
is the localization of
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})
at the class of
quasi-isomorphisms
, i.e. morphisms of dg
\mathcal{A}
-modules which induce quasi-isomorphisms of complexes when evaluated at any object in
\mathcal{A}
.
Let
\pi:\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
be the quotient functor.
For a morphism
j:M\rightarrow N
in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})
, we denote by
\overline{\jmath}=\pi(j):M\rightarrow N
the corresponding morphism in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
.
A dg functor
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
induces a triangle functor
F_{*}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{B})
which is an equivalence of triangulated categories if
F
is a quasi-equivalence.
The
dg derived category

\mathcal{D}_{dg}(\mathcal{A})
of
\mathcal{A}
is defined to be the full dg subcategory of
\mathcal{C}_{dg}(\mathcal{A})
consisting of cofibrant dg
\mathcal{A}
-modules in the
projective model structure
of
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})
(cf.
[
25
, Theorem 3.2]
).
The canonical functor
H^{0}(\mathcal{D}_{dg}(\mathcal{A}))\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
is an equivalence of triangulated categories.

For dg categories
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
, we define
\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
to be the full subcategory of
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{B}^{op})
whose objects are the dg bimodules
X
such that
X(-,B)
is quasi-representable for each object
B
of
\mathcal{B}
.
We define the canonical dg enhancement
\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
to be the full dg subcategory of
\mathcal{D}_{dg}(\mathcal{A}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{B}^{op})
whose objects are those of
\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
.

A dg category
\mathcal{A}
is
pretriangulated
if the canonical inclusion
H^{0}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
is a triangulated subcategory.
Note that
\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
is pretriangulated if
\mathcal{A}
is pretriangulated.
For a dg category
\mathcal{A}
, its
pretriangulated hull

\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})
(
[
4
,
8
,
5
]
) is defined as follows.
The objects of
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})
are “one-sided twisted complexes”, i.e. formal expressions
(\oplus_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}[r_{i}],q)
, where
A_{i}\in\mathcal{A}
,
r_{i}\in\mathbb{Z}
,
n\geq 0
,
q=(q_{ij})
,
q_{ij}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}^{r_{i}-r_{j}+1}(A_{j},A_{i})
,
q_{ij}=0
for
i\geq j
and
dq+q^{2}=0
.
Here we adopt the convention
(dq)_{ij}=(-1)^{r_{i}}d_{\mathcal{A}}(q_{ij})
and
(q^{2})_{ij}=\sum_{k}q_{ik}\circ q_{kj}
.
If
A
and
A^{\prime}
are two objects in
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})
with
A=(\oplus_{i=1}^{n}A_{i}[r_{i}],q)
and
A^{\prime}=(\oplus_{i^{\prime}=1}^{n^{\prime}}A_{i^{\prime}}^{\prime}[r_{i^{\prime}}^{\prime}],q^{\prime})
, the complex
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})}(A,A^{\prime})
has as the degree
m
component the space of matrices
f=(f_{ij})
,
f_{ij}\in\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}^{m+r_{i}^{\prime}-r_{j}}(A_{j},A_{i}^{\prime})
.
The differential
d
carries a morphism
f=(f_{ij})
of degree
m
to
df=((df)_{ij})
where

(df)_{ij}=(-1)^{r_{i}^{\prime}}d_{\mathcal{A}}(f_{ij})+\sum_{k}q^{\prime}_{ik}\circ f_{kj}-(-1)^{m}\sum_{k}f_{ik}\circ q_{kj}.

The composition map

\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})}(A^{\prime},A^{\prime\prime})\otimes\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})}(A,A^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})}(A,A^{\prime\prime})

is the matrix multiplication:
f^{\prime}\otimes f\mapsto f^{\prime\prime}
where
f^{\prime\prime}_{ij}=\sum_{k}f^{\prime}_{ik}\circ f_{kj}
.
We denote the triangulated category
H^{0}(\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A}))
by
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})
.
The Yoneda dg functor
\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}_{dg}(\mathcal{A})
extends to a fully faithful dg functor
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{dg}(\mathcal{A})
.
It induces a fully faithful triangle functor
\mathrm{tr}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
whose essential image is the triangulated subcategory of
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
generated by the representable dg modules.

A
quiver
(or
directed graph
)
Q
is a quadruple
(Q_{0},Q_{1},s,t)
where
Q_{0}
is a set whose elements are called
objects
or
vertices
of
Q
,
Q_{1}
is a set of
arrows

f
(or
edges
), and
s,t
are maps
Q_{1}\rightarrow Q_{0}
where
s(f)
is the
source
of the arrow
f
and
t(f)
is the
target
of
f
.
A morphism between quivers is defined in the obvious way.
We denote the category of quivers by
\mathrm{Quiv}
.

Each small category
\mathcal{C}
has an
underlying quiver

F(\mathcal{C})
and this extends to the forgetful functor
F:\mathrm{Cat}\rightarrow\mathrm{Quiv}
.
This functor admits a left adjoint
P:\mathrm{Quiv}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cat}
which associates to a quiver
Q
the
path category

P(Q)
of
Q
.
The path category
P(Q)
has the same objects as
Q
and its morphisms
x_{1}\rightarrow x_{n}
are finite paths

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces x_{n}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{n-1}}

consisting of
n\geq 1
objects
x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}
of
Q
which are connected by arrows
f_{i}:x_{i}\rightarrow x_{i+1}
of
Q
.
The composite of two paths is defined by concatenation:
\mathrm{concat}(f,g)=g\circ f
.

Let
Q
be a quiver and
R
a function which assigns to each pair of objects
x,y
of
Q
a binary relation
R_{x,y}
on the set of finite paths from
x
to
y
.
Then the path category of the quiver
Q
with relations
R
is defined to be the quotient category
P(Q)/\overline{R}
, where
\overline{R}
is the smallest family of equivalence relations
\overline{R}_{x,y}
,
x,y\in Q_{0}
, containing
R
and stable under pre- and postcomposition with morphisms.
Often we only write down nontrivial relations in
R
.

For a small category
\mathcal{I}
, we have the
k
-category
k\mathcal{I}
whose set of objects is the same as that of
\mathcal{I}
and for each pair of objects
x,y
in
\mathcal{C}
, the space
k\mathcal{I}(i,j)
is the free
k
-module generated by the set
\mathcal{I}(i,j)
.
In particular, if we view
k\mathcal{I}
as a dg category concentrated in degree 0, then it is
k
-cofibrant
, i.e. the Hom complexes are
k
-cofibrant.
Let
\mathcal{B}
be a
k
-cofibrant dg category.
We have a quasi-equivalence
\mathcal{A}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{B}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B}
.
A dg functor
F:\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}
gives rise to a
\mathcal{B}
-
\mathcal{A}
-bimodule
{}_{F}\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{A}}
defined by

(A,B)\mapsto\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(A,FB).

The assignment
F\mapsto{{}_{F}\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{A}}}
passes to the following map

\mathrm{Hqe}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathrm{Iso}(\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}))

which is a bijection, cf.
[
41
]
.
It is shown in loc. cit that
\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
is the internal Hom of the monoidal category
(\mathrm{Hqe},-\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}-)
, i.e. for small dg categories
\mathcal{A}
,
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{C}
, we have

\mathrm{Hqe}(\mathcal{A}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hqe}(\mathcal{A},\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{C})).

The following notion will be useful for the study of exact dg categories.

Definition 2.1
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be small dg categories and
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
a dg functor.
It is a connective quasi-equivalence if the induced dg functor
\tau_{\leq 0}F:\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{B}
is a quasi-equivalence.

Let
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\leq 0}
be the localization of
\mathrm{dgcat}
at the class of connective quasi-equivalences.
We denote by
\mathrm{dgcat}_{\leq 0}
the class of small and strictly connective dg categories.
and by
\mathcal{Q}{e}
the class of quasi-equivalences between connective dg categories.
A dg functor
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
in
\mathrm{dgcat}_{\leq 0}
is a
fibration

[
40
, Definition 3.8]
if:

\mathrm{(F1)_{\leq 0}}

for all objects
x
,
y
in
\mathcal{A}
, the morphism of connective complexes

F(x,y):\mathcal{A}(x,y)\rightarrow\mathcal{B}(x,y)

is surjective in negative degrees and,

\mathrm{(F2)_{\leq 0}}

given an object
x
in
\mathcal{A}
and a homotopy equivalence
v:Fx\rightarrow y
in
\mathcal{B}
, there exists a homotopy equivalence
x\rightarrow x^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{A}
, such that
F(u)=v
.

A dg functor
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
in
\mathrm{dgcat}_{\leq 0}
is a
cofibration

[
40
, Definition 3.9]
if it has the left lifting property with respect to the dg functors
\mathrm{dgcat}_{\leq 0}
which are simultaneous quasi-equivalences and fibrations.
By
[
40
, Theorem 3.10]
, the category
\mathrm{dgcat}_{\leq 0}
endowed with the above defined notions of weak equivalence, fibration, and cofibration, is a Quillen model category. Moreover, this model structure is cofibrantly generated, cf.
[
17
, 11.1.2]
.
Let
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\leq 0}^{\prime}
be the localization of
\mathrm{dgcat}_{\leq 0}
at the class of quasi-equivalences.
We have the following lemma, whose proof is similar to that of Lemma
3.6
.

Lemma 2.2
.

The adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{dgcat}_{\leq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{dgcat},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\leq 0}}

where the left adjoint
i:\mathrm{dgcat}_{\leq 0}\rightarrow\mathrm{dgcat}
is the inclusion functor and the right adjoint
\tau_{\leq 0}:\mathrm{dgcat}\rightarrow\mathrm{dgcat}_{\leq 0}
sends a small dg category
\mathcal{A}
to its connective cover
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}
, induces a pair of equivalences of categories which is quasi-inverse to each other

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hqe}_{\leq 0}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hqe}_{\leq 0}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\leq 0}}

2.2.
{A}_{\infty}
-categories

We recall some basic facts and notation for
{A}_{\infty}
-categories. Our standard references for
{A}_{\infty}
-categories are
[
22
,
23
,
24
,
30
]
. We follow the sign conventions of
[
15
]
. Note that we keep the assumption that
k
is a commutative ring.

Definition 2.3
.

An
{A}_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{A}
is the datum of

•

a class of objects
\mathrm{obj}(\mathcal{A})
,

•

for all
A,B\in\mathcal{A}
, a
\mathbb{Z}
-graded
k
-module
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(A,B)
, often denoted by
(A,B)
,

•

for all
n\geq 1
and all
A_{0}
, …,
A_{n}
, a graded map

m_{n}:(A_{n-1},A_{n})\otimes(A_{n-2},A_{n-1})\otimes\cdots\otimes(A_{0},A_{1})\rightarrow(A_{0},A_{n})

of degree
2-n

such that for each
n\geq 1
and all
A_{0},\ldots,A_{n}\in\mathcal{A}
, we have the identity

\sum(-1)^{r+st}m_{u}(\boldsymbol{1}^{\otimes r}\otimes m_{s}\otimes\boldsymbol{1}^{\otimes t})=0

of maps

(A_{n-1},A_{n})\otimes(A_{n-2},A_{n-1})\otimes\cdots\otimes(A_{0},A_{1})\rightarrow(A_{0},A_{n})

where the sum runs over all decompositions
n=r+s+t
and we put
u=r+1+t
.

Remark 2.4
.

The composition
m_{2}
induces an associative composition

\mu:H^{0}(\mathcal{A})(B,C)\otimes H^{0}(\mathcal{A})(A,B)\rightarrow H^{0}(\mathcal{A})(A,C)

for each triple of objects
(A,B,C)
in
\mathcal{A}
.

Definition 2.5
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an
{A}_{\infty}
-category and
A\in\mathcal{A}
. A morphism
e_{A}\in\mathrm{Hom}^{0}_{\mathcal{A}}(A,A)
is a strict identity if we have

m_{2}(f,e_{A})=f\mbox{ and }m_{2}(e_{A},g)=g

whenever these make sense and

m_{n}(\ldots,e_{A},\ldots)=0

for all
n\neq 2
.
In particular,
e_{A}
is a cycle of the complex
(\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(A,A),m_{1})
.
Clearly, if
e_{A}
exists, it is unique.
The
{A}_{\infty}
-category
\mathcal{A}
has strict identities if there is a strict identity
e_{A}
for each object
A\in\mathcal{A}
.

Definition 2.6
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be two
{A}_{\infty}
-categories. An
{A}_{\infty}
-functor
F:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow\mathcal{B}
is the datum of

•

a map
F:\mathrm{obj}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathrm{obj}(\mathcal{B})
,

•

for all
n\geq 1
and all
A_{0}
, …,
A_{n}\in\mathcal{A}
a graded map

F_{n}:(A_{n-1},A_{n})\otimes(A_{n-2},A_{n-1})\otimes\cdots\otimes(A_{0},A_{1})\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{B}}(FA_{0},FA_{n})

of degree
1-n

such that

\sum(-1)^{r+st}F_{u}(\boldsymbol{1}^{\otimes r}\otimes m_{s}\otimes\boldsymbol{1}^{\otimes t})=\sum(-1)^{s}m_{r}(F_{i_{1}}\otimes F_{i_{2}}\otimes\cdots\otimes F_{i_{r}})

where the first sum runs over all decompositions
n=r+s+t
, we put
u=r+1+t
, and the second sum runs over all
1\leq r\leq n
and all decompositions
n=i_{1}+\cdots+i_{r}
; the sign on the right hand side is given by

s=(r-1)(i_{1}-1)+(r-2)(i_{2}-1)+\cdots+2(i_{r-2}-1)+(i_{r-1}-1).

The
A_{\infty}
-functor
F
is strict if we have
F_{n}=0
for all
n\geq 2
. Clearly the above datum with
F_{n}=0
for
n\geq 2
defines a strict
A_{\infty}
-functor if and only if for each
k\geq 1
, we have

F_{1}m_{k}=m_{k}(F_{1}\otimes\cdots\otimes F_{1}).

Definition 2.7
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
and
\mathcal{B}
be two
{A}_{\infty}
-categories with strict identities. An
{A}_{\infty}
-functor
F
is strictly unital if

F_{1}(e_{A})=e_{FA}

and

F_{i}(\cdots\otimes e_{A}\otimes\cdots)=0

for all
i\geq 2
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a small
{A}_{\infty}
-category and
\mathcal{A}
an
{A}_{\infty}
-category.
It is known, cf.
[
29
]
, that there is a natural
{A}_{\infty}
-category
\mathrm{Fun}_{{A}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
of strictly unital
{A}_{\infty}
-functors between
\mathcal{B}
and
\mathcal{A}
.
It was constructed in
[
30
, 8.1.3]
using twists of
{A}_{\infty}
-structures.
Keller further developed an idea in
[
33
]
and interpreted it in
[
24
, 5.7]
as an internal
\mathrm{Hom}
-object in the tensor category of cocomplete augmented dg cocategories.
Note that when the target
\mathcal{A}
is a dg category, the
{A}_{\infty}
-category
\mathrm{Fun}_{\infty}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
is again a dg category.
The reason is that we use the
m_{j}^{\mathcal{A}}
,
j\geq i
, to define the map
m_{i}
of the
{A}_{\infty}
-category

\mathrm{Fun}_{{A}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
and when
\mathcal{A}
is a dg category, then the
m_{j}^{\mathcal{A}}
vanish for all
j\geq 3
.

Example 2.8
.

The dg category
\mathrm{Fun}_{{A}_{\infty}}(k\mathrm{Mor},\mathcal{A})
is isomorphic to the dg morphism category
\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{A})
introduced in
[
8
, 2.9]
. More precisely,
\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{A})
has as objects the closed morphisms
f:A\rightarrow B
of degree 0 in
\mathcal{A}
. The morphism complex
\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{A})((A,B,f),(A^{\prime},B^{\prime},f^{\prime}))
has as the degree
m
component the space of matrices

\begin{pmatrix}j&0\\
h&l\end{pmatrix}

where the morphisms
j:A\rightarrow A^{\prime}
,
l:B\rightarrow B^{\prime}
are of degree
m
and the morphism
h:A\rightarrow B^{\prime}
is of degree
m-1
. The differential
d
carries a morphism

\begin{pmatrix}j&0\\
h&l\end{pmatrix}

to

\begin{pmatrix}-d(j)&0\\
d(h)+f^{\prime}j-(-1)^{m}lf&d(l)\end{pmatrix}
.
The composition map is the multiplication of matrices.

2.3.
Homotopy diagrams

Throughout we fix a dg
k
-category
\mathcal{A}
.
Our first aim is to formulate proper definitions of homotopy (co)cartesian squares in the dg category
\mathcal{A}
(not
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
).

We are mainly concerned with the case when the small category
\mathcal{I}
is the category with one object and one morphism or one of the following categories:

The
square
category
\mathrm{Sq}
is the path category of the quiver

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 00}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 01}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 10}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 11}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}

with the commutativity relation
jf\sim kg
;

the
cospan
category
\mathrm{Cosp}
is the path category of the quiver

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 01}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 10}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 11;}

the
span
category
\mathrm{Sp}
is the path category of the quiver

the
composition
category
\mathrm{Com}
is the path category of the quiver

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2;}

the
morphism
category
\mathrm{Mor}
is the path category of the quiver

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1.}

We identify objects
X\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k(\mathrm{Sq}^{op}))
with commutative squares

(2.8.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{00}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{01}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{10}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{11}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k}

in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})
.

In general we identify an object
X\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathcal{I}^{op})
with an
\mathcal{I}
-shaped diagram
X
in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})
, i.e. a functor

X:\mathcal{I}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A}),\;\;i\mapsto X_{i}.

Let
Q:\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
be the canonical quotient functor.
The map sending
X
to
Q\circ X
defines the canonical
diagram functor

\mathrm{Dia}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathcal{I}^{op})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}))
which is
conservative
, i.e. detects isomorphisms. Note that it is not full in general.

We have the following diagram with the obvious functors
i:\mathrm{Cosp}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}
and
s:\mathrm{Mor}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cosp}
.

Let us describe the functor
R
.
Let

be the object

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

.

It fits into a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}(k\mathrm{Cosp}^{op})

0\rightarrow 11^{\wedge}\rightarrow 01^{\wedge}\oplus 10^{\wedge}\rightarrow M(-,00)\rightarrow 0.

So it is quasi-isomorphic to the cokernel
N
of the graded-injective map

11^{\wedge}\rightarrow\mathrm{Cone}(\mathrm{id}_{11^{\wedge}})\oplus 10^{\wedge}\oplus 01^{\wedge}.

Let
M^{\prime}
be the dg
k(\mathrm{Sq})\otimes k\mathrm{Cosp}^{op}
-module given by the following diagram in
\mathcal{C}(k\mathrm{Cosp}^{op})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 10^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 01^{\wedge}\oplus\mathrm{Cone}(\mathrm{id}_{11^{\wedge}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 11^{\wedge}\mathrlap{\;.}}

Then we get the desired quasi-isomorphism
M^{\prime}\rightarrow M
.
So the functor
R
takes
S
to
\mathrm{Hom}_{k\mathrm{Cosp}^{op}}(M^{\prime},S)
which is given by the following diagram in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C\oplus\mathrm{Cone}(-\mathrm{id}_{\Sigma^{-1}D})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(k\ p)}

such that
p:\mathrm{Cone}(-\mathrm{id}_{\Sigma^{-1}D})\rightarrow D
is the canonical projection map and that the diagram fits into the following graded split short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})

0\rightarrow K\rightarrow\mathrm{Cone}(-\mathrm{id}_{\Sigma^{-1}D})\oplus B\oplus C\xrightarrow{[p\;\;j\;\;k]}D\rightarrow 0.

In summary, we have the following diagram of triangle functors

(2.8.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Mor}^{op})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Cosp}^{op})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Sq}^{op}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}

Lemma 2.9

(
[
27
,
3
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a dg category.
There is a canonical t-structure

(\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\leq 0},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\geq 0})

on
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
such that
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\geq 0}
is formed by those dg modules whose cohomology is concentrated in non-negative degrees.

Note that we have
A^{\wedge}\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\leq 0}
for each
A\in\mathcal{A}
.
Note also that when
\mathcal{A}
is connective, then
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\leq 0}
is formed by those dg modules whose cohomology is concentrated in non-positive degrees.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a connective dg
k
-category.
Put
\mathcal{T}=\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{B}^{op})
.
Let
(\mathcal{T}^{\leq 0},\mathcal{T}^{\geq 0})
be the canonical t-structure on
\mathcal{T}
.
Let
X
be a dg
\mathcal{B}
-
\mathcal{A}
-bimodule. It is clear that
X\in\mathcal{T}^{\geq 0}
if and only if
X(-,B)\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\geq 0}
for each
B\in\mathcal{B}
.

Lemma 2.10
.

We have
X\in\mathcal{T}^{\leq 0}
if
X(-,B)\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\leq 0}
for each
B\in\mathcal{B}
.

Proof.

Suppose
X(-,B)\in\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})^{\leq 0}
for each
B\in\mathcal{B}
. Let
Y\in\mathcal{T}^{\geq 1}
.
Then we have
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})}(X(-,B),Y(-,B^{\prime}))=0
for
B,B^{\prime}\in\mathcal{B}
.
So, we have
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y)=0
. Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B}^{op}}(X,Y)=

\displaystyle\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathcal{B}^{op}}(\mathcal{B},\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{B}\otimes\mathcal{B}^{op}}(\mathcal{B},\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}_{\mathcal{A}}(X,Y))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 0.

Hence
X\in\mathcal{T}^{\leq 0}
.
∎

In particular, for a small category
\mathcal{I}
, the category
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathcal{I},\mathcal{A})
is a full subcategory of
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathcal{I}^{op})^{\leq 0}
.
Recall that for a small category
\mathcal{I}
, the canonical diagram functor

\mathrm{Dia}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathcal{I}^{op})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}({\mathcal{A}}))

sends an object
X:\mathcal{I}\rightarrow\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})
to the corresponding diagram
\mathrm{Dia}(X)=\pi\circ X
where
\pi:\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
is the canonical quotient functor.
Recall also that, for a category
\mathcal{C}
, we denote by
\mathrm{Iso}(\mathcal{C})
the class of isomorphism classes of objects in
\mathcal{C}
.
The following lemma is well-known.

Lemma 2.11
.

Let
\mathcal{I}
be the path category
P(Q)
of a quiver
Q
(without relations).
The functor
\mathrm{Dia}:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathcal{I}^{op})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}))
is an epivalence, i.e. it is full and dense and detects isomorphisms.
In particular, it induces a bijection

\mathrm{Iso}(\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathcal{I}^{op}))\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathrm{Iso}(\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{I},\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}))).

3.
Homotopy pullbacks and homotopy pushouts

In this section, we introduce the notions of homotopy (co)cartesian square over a dg
k
-category
\mathcal{A}
using the category
\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
of representations up to homotopy, where
\mathcal{B}
is a certain dg category. We then introduce the category
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})
of
3
-term homotopy complexes over
\mathcal{A}
, using (strictly unital)
{A}_{\infty}
-functors.
Building upon a result of Canonaco–Ornaghi–Stellari and Faonte concerning the dg category
\mathrm{Fun}_{{A}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
of
{A}_{\infty}
-functors and
\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
, we interpret the notions of homotopy (co)cartesian square in
\mathcal{A}
into the notions of homotopy left (right) exact sequence in
\mathcal{A}
.
Additionally, we establish several diagram lemmas that will prove useful in the subsequent sections.

3.1.
Homotopy pullbacks and homotopy pushouts

Definition 3.1
.

An object
X\in\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
is a
homotopy cartesian square
with respect to
\mathcal{A}
if the canonical map
X_{00}\rightarrow\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{Cone}((-j,k))
induces an isomorphism

\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}(A^{\wedge},X_{00})\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}(A^{\wedge},\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{Cone}((-j,k)))

in
\mathcal{D}(k)
for each
A
in
\mathcal{A}
.

Remark 3.2
.

Let
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
be a full dg subcategory of
\mathcal{A}
.
Let
F:\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A}^{\prime})\rightarrow\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
be the inclusion functor induced by the inclusion dg functor
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}
.
Let
X
be an object in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A}^{\prime})
.
If
F(X)
is homotopy cartesian with respect to
\mathcal{A}
, then
X
is homotopy cartesian with respect to
\mathcal{A}^{\prime}
.

In the rest of the chapter, we will only use this relative version of homotopy (co)cartesian squares.

For an object
X\in\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
, by Lemma
2.10
, the unit morphism
X\rightarrow RLX
induces a canonical morphism
X\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}RLX
in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Sq}^{op})
.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
X
be an object in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
. If the canonical map
X\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}RLX
is an isomorphism, then
X
is homotopy cartesian. If
\mathcal{A}
is a connective dg category, then the converse also holds.

Proof.

Let
X
be the object in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
corresponding to the following commutative square in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})

Let
p:\mathrm{Cone}(-\mathrm{id}_{\Sigma^{-1}D})\rightarrow D
be the canonical projection.

X\rightarrow RLX
can be described by the following commutative diagram in

:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}f\\

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\begin{bmatrix}0\ 1\ 0\\
0\ 0\ 1\end{bmatrix}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}-j{,}k{]}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}p{,}-j{,}k{]}}

where the second row is a short exact sequence in
\mathcal{C}(\mathcal{A})
.
By Lemma
2.10
, we find that
(\tau_{\leq 0}RLX)_{00}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\tau_{\leq 0}K
.
We also have a canonical commutative diagram in

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-34.45464pt}{-15.94699pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

The map
u
induces a canonical map
A\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}K
in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
.
It is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
if and only if the canonical map
X\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}RLX
is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Sq}^{op})
.

The object
X
is a homotopy cartesian square if and only if for each object
A^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{A}
, the canonical map
u:A\rightarrow K
induces an isomorphism in
\mathcal{D}(k)

\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}(A^{\prime\wedge},A)\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}(A^{\prime\wedge},K).

Note that we have

\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}(A^{\prime\wedge},\tau_{\leq 0}K)\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}(A^{\prime\wedge},K).

So if the canonical map
A\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}K
is an isomorphism in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
, then
X
is homotopy cartesian.

When the dg category
\mathcal{A}
is connective, we have

\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}(A^{\prime\wedge},A)\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{RHom}(A^{\prime\wedge},A)

and

\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}(A^{\prime\wedge},\tau_{\leq 0}K)\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{RHom}(A^{\prime\wedge},\tau_{\leq 0}K).

So the converse also holds.

∎

Example 3.4
.

Each graded-split short exact sequence
0\rightarrow A\xrightarrow{f}B\xrightarrow{j}C\rightarrow 0
in
\mathcal{A}
gives rise to a homotopy cartesian square

\hbox to90.1pt{\vbox to51.06pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 45.04875pt\lower-24.03725pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-45.04875pt}{-20.37752pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{64}\lxSVG@closescope }}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}}

A^{\wedge}\rightarrow\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{Cone}(j^{\wedge})
is already a quasi-isomorphism.

Example 3.5
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be an additive category which we consider as a dg category concentrated in degree zero.
Then the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\wedge}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\wedge}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\wedge}}

with
A,B,C\in\mathcal{A}
is homotopy cartesian if and only if in the sequence

f
is a kernel of
j
. Indeed, we identify the pretriangulated hull
\mathrm{pretr}(\mathcal{A})
of
\mathcal{A}
with the dg category
\mathcal{C}^{b}_{dg}(\mathcal{A})
of bounded complexes over
\mathcal{A}
.
Then the object
\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{Cone}(j^{\wedge})
is identified with the complex
\ldots\rightarrow 0\rightarrow B\rightarrow C\rightarrow 0\rightarrow\ldots
where
B
is in degree
0
.
For each
A^{\prime}\in\mathcal{A}
, the space
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{Hom}(A^{\prime\wedge},\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{Cone}(j^{\wedge}))
is then identified with the kernel of the map
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(A^{\prime},B)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{A}}(A^{\prime},C)
.
The conclusion follows at once.

The following lemma was obtained independently by Genovese, Lowen and Van den Bergh, c.f.
[
14
, Lemma 2.5.5]
.

Lemma 3.6
.

Let
\mathcal{B}
be a connective dg category. Then the natural functor

F:\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})

is an equivalence of categories.
Let
H:\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{B}^{op})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{B}^{op})
be the restriction functor.
Then a quasi-inverse of
F
is given by
G=\tau_{\leq 0}\circ H
.
Moreover, for any
X,Y\in\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A})
, we have
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}(X,Y)\xrightarrow{\sim}\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}(FX,FY)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathrm{dgcat}_{\leq 0}
be the category of small strictly connective dg categories.
Then we have the following adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{dgcat}_{\leq 0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{dgcat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\leq 0}}

where the left adjoint
i:\mathrm{dgcat}_{\leq 0}\rightarrow\mathrm{dgcat}
is the inclusion functor and the right adjoint
\tau_{\leq 0}:\mathrm{dgcat}\rightarrow\mathrm{dgcat}_{\leq 0}
sends a small dg category
\mathcal{A}
to
\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}
.

Let
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\leq 0}^{\prime}
be the localization of
\mathrm{dgcat}_{\leq 0}
at the class of quasi-equivalences of strictly connective dg categories.
The functors
i
and
\tau_{\leq 0}
clearly preserve quasi-equivalences.
Hence by the universal properties of localizations of categories, the above adjunction induces the following adjunction

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hqe}_{\leq 0}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hqe}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\leq 0}}

where
i
is fully faithful.

We claim that the canonical morphism in
\mathrm{Hqe}_{\leq 0}

\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(\mathcal{B},\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})

is an isomorphism.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be an arbitrary small strictly connective dg category.
Recall that the tensor product of strictly connective dg categories remains strictly connective, and
that each connective dg category admits a cofibrant replacement which is strictly connective.
Recall also that
\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
is the internal Hom of the monoidal category
(\mathrm{Hqe},-\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}-)
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Hqe}_{\leq 0}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C},\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(i(\mathcal{B}),i(\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A})))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Hqe}}(i(\mathcal{C}),\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(i(\mathcal{B}),i(\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A})))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Hqe}}(i(\mathcal{C})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}i(\mathcal{B}),i(\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Hqe}_{\leq 0}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C}\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}\mathcal{B},\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Hqe}}(i(\mathcal{C})\otimes^{\mathbb{L}}i(\mathcal{B}),\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Hqe}}(i(\mathcal{C}),\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(i(\mathcal{B}),\mathcal{A}))

\displaystyle=\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathrm{Hqe}_{\leq 0}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{C},\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(i(\mathcal{B}),\mathcal{A})).

∎

The following lemma allows us to reduce, in many cases, to the connective case when considering homotopy (co)cartesian squares.

Lemma 3.7
.

Consider the equivalence of categories
F:\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
as in Lemma
3.6
.
An object
X
in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A})
is homotopy cartesian
if and only if its image under
F
in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
is homotopy cartesian.

Proof.

be the commutative square of quasi-representable dg

-modules given by the diagram (
2.8.1
).

By applying
\mathrm{Hom}_{\mathcal{D}(\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A})}(A^{\wedge},-)
to the above triangle for each
A\in\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A}
, we get the associated long exact sequence

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}(A^{\wedge},X_{00})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}(A^{\wedge},\Sigma^{-1}X_{11})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}(A^{\wedge},\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{Cone}(\psi))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Hom}(A^{\wedge},X_{10}\oplus X_{01})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\dots}

We have
\mathrm{Hom}(A^{\wedge},\Sigma^{-n}X_{ij})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hom}(FA^{\wedge},\Sigma^{-n}FX_{ij})
for
n\geq 0
, where
F:\mathcal{D}(\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A})
is the induction functor.
By the Five-Lemma, it is then clear that an object
X
in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\tau_{\leq 0}\mathcal{A})
is homotopy cartesian if and only if its image under
F
in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
is homotopy cartesian.
∎

Definition 3.8
.

An object
S
in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Cosp},\mathcal{A})
is said to admit a homotopy pullback if there exists a homotopy cartesian square
X
in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
with an isomorphism
\varphi:LX\rightarrow S
in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Cosp}^{op})
.
In this case, the pair
(X,\varphi)
is called a homotopy pullback of
S
.
We will also abuse the notation and say that
X
is a homotopy pullback of
S
.

Remark 3.9
.

The isomorphism
\varphi:LX\rightarrow S
in the definition of a homotopy pullback is in general not the identity.
For example, let
\mathcal{A}
be the dg category of two-term complexes of finitely generated projective modules over a finite dimensional algebra
A
.
The cospan

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Cone}(\mathrm{id}_{A})}

where
A
denotes the corresponding stalk complex, does not admit a homotopy pullback
(X,\varphi)
with
\varphi
being the identity.
It admits a homotopy pullback with
X
given by

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

and
\varphi^{-1}
given by the obvious morphism

Obviously, the restriction dg functor
L:\mathcal{D}_{dg}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Sq}^{op})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{dg}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Cosp}^{op})
restricts to a dgfunctor
L:\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(k\mathrm{Cosp},\mathcal{A})
.

Proposition 3.10
.

The dg functor
\tau_{\leq 0}L:\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(k\mathrm{Cosp},\mathcal{A})
restricted to
the full subcategory consisting of homotopy cartesian squares is quasi-fully faithful.
In particular, the homotopy pullback of an object
S
in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Cosp},\mathcal{A})
,
if it exists, is unique up to a unique isomorphism.

Proof.

By Lemmas
3.6
and
3.7
, we may assume that the dg category
\mathcal{A}
is connective.
Let
X
and
X^{\prime}
be two homotopy cartesian squares.

Let
f:\Sigma^{i}LX\rightarrow LX^{\prime}
be a morphism in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Cosp}^{op})
, where
i\geq 0
.
From the adjunction

we have a morphism
g:\Sigma^{i}X\rightarrow RLX^{\prime}
.
Since the object
X^{\prime}
is homotopy cartesian, by Lemma
3.3
,
the map
\Sigma^{i}X\rightarrow RLX^{\prime}
induces a morphism
h:\Sigma^{i}X\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}RLX^{\prime}\xleftarrow{\sim}X^{\prime}
.
It is easy to see that
L(h)=f
.

Let
h:\Sigma^{i}X\rightarrow X^{\prime}
be a morphism in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Sq}^{op})
, where
i\geq 0
.
We have the following diagram in
\mathcal{D}(\mathrm{Sq})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{i}X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\leq 0}(\Sigma^{i}RLX)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sigma^{i}RLX}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tau_{\leq 0}RLX^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces RLX^{\prime}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces RL(h)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Therefore, if the morphism
L(h)
is zero, then
h
is also zero.

∎

Remark 3.11

(Universal property of homotopy pullbacks)
.

Similarly, one can show that given a homotopy cartesian square
X^{\prime}
and an object
X
in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
, if we have a map
\varphi:LX\rightarrow LX^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Cosp},\mathcal{A})
, then we have a canonical map
\theta:X\rightarrow X^{\prime}
in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
such that
L(\theta)=\varphi
.

Recall that we have the pair of duality functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{rep}(k\mathcal{I},\mathcal{A})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{rep}(k\mathcal{I}^{op},\mathcal{A}^{op})^{op}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{RHom}(-\text{,}\mathcal{A})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{RHom}(-\text{,}\mathcal{A})}

Then we can form the dual definitions of a homotopy cocartesian square with respect to
\mathcal{A}
and of a homotopy pushout of an object in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Sp}^{op})
.

Definition 3.12
.

An object
X\in\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
given by the diagram (
2.8.1
) is a
homotopy

cocartesian square with respect to
\mathcal{A}
if the canonical map
\mathrm{Cone}((f,g)^{\intercal})\rightarrow X_{11}
induces an isomorphism in
\mathcal{D}(k)

\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}(X_{11},A^{\wedge})\rightarrow\tau_{\leq 0}\mathrm{RHom}(\mathrm{Cone}((f,g)^{\intercal}),A^{\wedge})

for each
A
in
\mathcal{A}
.

Consider the adjunction
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Sp}^{op})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Sq}^{op})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces L}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R}

induced by the inclusion
\mathrm{Sp}\rightarrow\mathrm{Sq}
.

Definition 3.13
.

An object
S
in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sp},\mathcal{A})
is said to admit a homotopy pushout if there is a homotopy cartesian square
X
in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
with an isomorphism
\varphi:S\rightarrow RX
in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Sp}^{op})
. In this case, the pair
(X,\varphi)
is called a homotopy pushout of
S
. We will also abuse the notation and say that
X
is a homotopy pushout of
S
.

3.2.
An
A_{\infty}
-theoretic description

Our main aim in this subsection is to provide a description of the
homotopy
category
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})
of homotopy
3
-term complexes
using the
{A}_{\infty}
-formalism, cf. Definition
3.14
.
Building upon Theorem
3.16
, we establish the existence of a canonical fully faithful functor
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})\hookrightarrow\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
, as elaborated in Lemma
3.17
.
Additionally, for later use, we introduce the category
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Cosp}}(\mathcal{A})
of homotopy cospans and the category
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Sq}}(\mathcal{A})
of homotopy squares in
\mathcal{A}
, detailed in Definitions
3.24
and
3.21
.
They are equivalent to the categories
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Cosp},\mathcal{A})
and
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
, respectively.
In the remaining part of this subsection, we interpret the definitions from the previous subsection using these notions.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a dg category and
\mathcal{B}
the dg
k
-path category of the following graded quiver with relations

(3.13.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}

where
|a|=|b|=0
,
d(a)=0
,
d(b)=0
and
ba=0
.

Our aim is to describe the dg category
\mathrm{Fun}_{{A}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
following the construction in
[
30
]
. It has strictly unital
{A}_{\infty}
-functors as its objects and strictly unital morphisms as its morphisms.
We identify a strictly unital
{A}_{\infty}
-functor
F:\mathcal{B}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}
with the corresponding diagram in
\mathcal{A}

(3.13.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

where
|f|=|j|=0
,
|h|=-1
and
d(f)=0
,
d(j)=0
and
d(h)=-jf
.
Let
F
and
F^{\prime}
be two strictly unital
{A}_{\infty}
-functors from
\mathcal{B}
to
\mathcal{A}
.
We identify them with their corresponding diagrams.
A strictly unital morphism
H:F\rightarrow F^{\prime}
of degree
n
is identified with the following diagram

(3.13.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\prime}}

where
|r_{i}|=n
for
i=0,1,2
,
|s_{1}|=|s_{2}|=n-1
,
|t|=n-2
.
Let
H\in\mathrm{Hom}^{n}(F,F^{\prime})
. Put
H^{\prime}=d(H)\in\mathrm{Hom}^{n+1}(F,F^{\prime})
.
Through the identification with diagrams, we observe that the corresponding diagram for
d(H)
is as follows:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{0}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{1}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces t^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{1}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s_{2}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces r_{2}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\prime}}

where

r_{i}^{\prime}=d(r_{i})

for
i=0,1,2
, and

s_{1}^{\prime}=d(s_{1})+(-1)^{n+1}(f^{\prime}\circ r_{0})+(-1)^{n}r_{1}\circ f,

s_{2}^{\prime}=d(s_{2})+(-1)^{n+1}(j^{\prime}\circ r_{1})+(-1)^{n}r_{2}\circ j,

and

t^{\prime}=d(t)+(-1)^{n}(j^{\prime}\circ s_{1})+h^{\prime}\circ r_{0}+(-1)^{n}(s_{2}\circ f)+(-1)^{n+1}(r_{2}\circ h).

Clearly we have
d^{2}(H)=0
.

We now describe the compositions.
Let
F
,
F^{\prime}
and
F^{\prime\prime}
be strictly unital
{A}_{\infty}
-functors from
\mathcal{B}
to
\mathcal{A}
. Let
H^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Hom}^{r}(F,F^{\prime})
and
H^{\prime\prime}\in\mathrm{Hom}^{s}(F^{\prime},F^{\prime\prime})
be two morphisms in the dg category
\mathrm{Fun}_{{A}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
.
Assume the corresponding diagram is given by

\hbox to165.67pt{\vbox to133.2pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 82.83325pt\lower-67.14442pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

where

\widetilde{r_{i}}=r_{i}^{\prime\prime}r_{i}^{\prime}

for
i=1,2,3
, and

\widetilde{s_{1}}=r_{1}^{\prime\prime}s_{1}^{\prime}+(-1)^{r}s_{1}^{\prime\prime}r_{0}^{\prime},

\widetilde{s_{2}}=r_{2}^{\prime\prime}s_{2}^{\prime}+(-1)^{r}s_{2}^{\prime\prime}r_{1}^{\prime},

and

\widetilde{t}=r_{2}^{\prime\prime}t^{\prime}+t^{\prime\prime}r_{0}^{\prime}+(-1)^{r+1}s_{2}^{\prime\prime}s_{1}^{\prime}.

Note that in the expression of
\widetilde{t}
, we only have length
2
paths with suitable signs.
This is due to the vanishing of
m_{j}^{\mathcal{A}}
for
j\geq 3
.

Definition 3.14
.

Let
\mathcal{A}
be a dg category.
The homotopy category
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})
of 3-term homotopy complexes (= 3-term
h
-complexes, or 3-term complexes up to homotopy) over
\mathcal{A}
is defined to be
H^{0}\mathrm{Fun}_{{A}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})
where
\mathcal{B}
is the dg category (
3.13.1
).
More precisely, its objects are given by diagrams
X
in
\mathcal{A}
of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

where
|f|=|j|=0
,
|h|=-1
and
d(f)=0
,
d(j)=0
and
d(h)=-jf
.

Suppose
X^{\prime}
is given by the diagram with objects and morphisms with superscript a prime symbol.

A morphism from
X
to
X^{\prime}
is given by a homotopy equivalence class of 6-tuples
(r_{i},s_{j},t)
,
i=0,1,2
,
j=1,2
where

r_{i}:A_{i}\rightarrow A_{i}^{\prime},|r_{i}|=0,d(r_{i})=0,

s_{j}:A_{j-1}\rightarrow A_{j}^{\prime},|s_{j}|=-1,d(s_{1})=f^{\prime}r_{0}-r_{1}f,

(3.14.1)

d(s_{2})=j^{\prime}r_{1}-r_{2}j,

and

(3.14.2)

t:A_{0}\rightarrow A_{2}^{\prime},|t|=-2,d(t)=r_{2}\circ h-h^{\prime}\circ r_{0}-s_{2}\circ f-j^{\prime}\circ s_{1}.

Two 6-tuples
(r_{i},s_{j},t)
and
(r_{i}^{\prime},s_{j}^{\prime},t^{\prime})
are homotopy equivalent if there exists a 6-tuple
(r^{\prime\prime}_{i},s_{j}^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime})
such that

|r_{i}^{\prime\prime}|=-1,r_{i}-r_{i}^{\prime}=d(r_{i}^{\prime\prime}),

|s_{j}^{\prime\prime}|=-2,s_{1}-s_{1}^{\prime}=d(s_{1}^{\prime\prime})+f^{\prime}\circ r_{0}^{\prime\prime}-r_{1}^{\prime\prime}\circ f,

s_{2}-s_{2}^{\prime}=d(s_{2}^{\prime\prime})+j^{\prime}\circ r_{1}^{\prime\prime}-r_{2}^{\prime\prime}\circ j,

and

|t^{\prime\prime}|=-3,t-t^{\prime}=d(t^{\prime\prime})-j^{\prime}\circ s_{1}^{\prime\prime}+h^{\prime}\circ r_{0}^{\prime\prime}-s_{2}^{\prime\prime}\circ f+r_{2}^{\prime\prime}\circ h.

The composition of two morphisms given by homotopy equivalence classes of 6-tuples
(r_{i}^{\prime},s_{j}^{\prime},t^{\prime}):F\rightarrow F^{\prime}
and
(r_{i}^{\prime\prime},s_{j}^{\prime\prime},t^{\prime\prime}):F^{\prime}\rightarrow F^{\prime}
, is the morphism given by the 6-tuple
(\widetilde{r}_{i},\widetilde{s}_{j},\widetilde{t}):F\rightarrow F^{\prime\prime}
where

\displaystyle\widetilde{r}_{i}

\displaystyle=r_{i}^{\prime\prime}r_{i}^{\prime},

\displaystyle\widetilde{s}_{1}

\displaystyle=r_{1}^{\prime\prime}s_{1}^{\prime}+s_{1}^{\prime\prime}r_{0}^{\prime},

\displaystyle\widetilde{s}_{2}

\displaystyle=r_{2}^{\prime\prime}s_{2}^{\prime}+s_{2}^{\prime\prime}r_{1}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\widetilde{t}

\displaystyle=r_{2}^{\prime\prime}t^{\prime}+t^{\prime\prime}r_{0}^{\prime}-s_{2}^{\prime\prime}s_{1}^{\prime}.

The identity morphism for
X
is given by the homotopy equivalence class of the 6-tuple
(r_{i},s_{j},t)
where
r_{i}=\mathrm{id}
,
s_{j}=0
and
t=0
for
i=0,1,2
and
j=1,2
.

From the definition, we see that
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})
is isomorphic to
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\tau_{\leq 0}(\mathcal{A}))
.
Thus we may assume
\mathcal{A}
is strictly connective when needed.

Clearly if a 6-tuple
(r_{i},s_{j},t)
gives rise to an isomorphism in
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})
, then
r_{i}
is a homotopy equivalence for each
i=0,1,2
.
The converse is also true by the following proposition.

Proposition 3.15

(
[
30
]
, Proposition 8.2.2.3)
.

Let
\theta:F\rightarrow F^{\prime}
be a morphism in
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})
given by the homotopy equivalence class of a 6-tuple
(r_{i},s_{j},t)
as above.
If
r_{i}
is a homotopy equivalence in
\mathcal{A}
for each
i=0,1,2
, then
\theta
is an isomorphism.

The following theorem is claimed by Kontsevich, modulo some results by Toën.

Theorem 3.16

(
[
6
]
cf. also
[
13
]
)
.

We have a canonical isomorphism

\mathrm{Fun}_{{A}_{\infty}}(-,-)\rightarrow\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(-,-)

of bifunctors
\mathrm{Hqe}_{k-\mathrm{cf}}^{op}\times\mathrm{Hqe}\rightarrow\mathrm{Hqe}
, where
\mathrm{Hqe}_{k-\mathrm{cf}}
is the
full subcategory of
\mathrm{Hqe}
whose objects are the dg categories whose morphism
complexes are cofibrant dg
k
-modules.

Denote by
\widetilde{\mathrm{Com}}
the dg path
k
-category of the quiver

(3.16.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 2}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}

where
{|}a{|}=0
,
{|}b{|}=0
,
{|}c{|}=-1
, and
d(c)=ba
.
We identify objects in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes\widetilde{\mathrm{Com}}^{op})
with sequences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

in
\mathcal{C}_{dg}(\mathcal{A})
where
f
and
j
are closed morphisms of degree 0 and
h
is a morphism of degree
-1
such that
d(h)=jf
.

Denote by
\widetilde{\mathrm{Sq}}
the dg quotient of

by its full dg subcategory consisting of the object 10, i.e. the dg path
k
-category of the quiver

(3.16.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 00}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 01}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 10}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 11}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}

where
{|}f{|}=0={|}j{|}={|}g{|}={|}l{|}
,
{|}h{|}=-1
,
d(h)=\mathrm{id}_{10}
, and
jf=lg
.
We identify objects in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes\widetilde{\mathrm{Sq}}^{op})
with diagrams

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces l}

in
\mathcal{C}_{dg}(\mathcal{A})
where
f,g,j,l
are closed morphisms of degree 0 such that
jf=lg
and
h
is a morphism of degree
-1
such that
d(h)=\mathrm{id}_{N}
.

Let
F:\widetilde{\mathrm{Com}}\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathrm{Sq}}

,

By
[
8
, Proposition 1.6.3, Proposition 4.6]
, the dg functor
\mathrm{id}\otimes G^{op}:\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Sq}^{op}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}\otimes\widetilde{\mathrm{Sq}}^{op}
is a dg quotient of
\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Sq}^{op}
modulo
\mathcal{A}\otimes\{01\}
and hence a localization functor, i.e. the restriction functor along
\mathrm{id}\otimes G^{op}
is fully faithful.
Let
\mathcal{S}
be the right orthogonal of the objects
(A,01)^{\wedge}
in
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Sq}^{op})
, where
A
runs through objects in
\mathcal{A}
.
More precisely,
\mathcal{S}
is the full triangulated subcategory of
\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Sq}^{op})
consisting of squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}

where
N
is an acyclic dg
\mathcal{A}
-module.

Lemma 3.17
.

F
is a quasi-equivalence and
G
induces an equivalence
\mathcal{S}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes\widetilde{\mathrm{Sq}}^{op})
.

Proof.

It is enough to show that
F
induces a quasi-isomorphism of complexes

\mathrm{Hom}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{Com}}}(0,2)\rightarrow\mathrm{Hom}_{\widetilde{\mathrm{Sq}}}(00,11).

On the left hand side, the complex is given by

\cdots\rightarrow 0\rightarrow k\cdot c\xrightarrow{\sim}k\cdot(ba)\rightarrow 0\rightarrow\cdots,

which is contractible. On the right hand side, the complex is given by

\cdots\rightarrow k\cdot(lh^{3}g)\xrightarrow{\sim}k\cdot(lh^{2}g)\xrightarrow{0}k\cdot(lhg)\xrightarrow{\sim}k\cdot(lg)\rightarrow 0\rightarrow\cdots,

which is also contractible.
Thus
F
is a quasi-equivalence.
The rest follows from
[
8
, Proposition 4.6 (ii)]
.
∎

As a consequence of Lemma
3.17
, we have a fully faithful functor

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes\widetilde{\mathrm{Com}}^{op})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{D}(\mathcal{A}\otimes k\mathrm{Sq}^{op}),}

which sends an object

(
\bigstar\bigstar
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

to

(
\bigstar\bigstar\bigstar
)

Recall that the homotopy category
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})
of 3-term h-complexes over
\mathcal{A}
is defined to be
H^{0}(\mathrm{Fun}_{{A}_{\infty}}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A}))
, where
\mathcal{B}
is the dg category defined by (
3.13.1
).
We have an obvious quasi-equivalence
\widetilde{\mathrm{Com}}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathcal{B}
.
Hence, by Theorem
3.16
, we have a fully faithful functor

F:\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{rep}(\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{rep}(\widetilde{\mathrm{Com}},\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A}),

sending a 3-term h-complex over
\mathcal{A}

(3.17.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{2},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

where
|f|=|j|=0
,
|h|=-1
, and
d(f)=0
,
d(j)=0
, and
d(h)=-jf
, to the following object in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})

(3.17.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}^{\wedge}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces IA_{0}^{\wedge}\;\;}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\;\;A_{2}^{\wedge}\mathrlap{.}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\wedge}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\wedge}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}-h^{\wedge}{,}-d(h^{\wedge}){]}}

Definition 3.18
.

A 3-term h-complex over
\mathcal{A}
, as given by (
3.17.1
), is a homotopy (left, right) short exact sequence if the corresponding object in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
, as given by (
3.17.2
), is homotopy bicartesian (cartesian, cocartesian).

Remark 3.19
.

The properties of being homotopy left exact, homotopy right exact, or homotopy short exact are invariant under isomorphisms in
\mathcal{H}_{3t}(\mathcal{A})
.

Let
j:V\rightarrow W
be a morphism of complexes of
k
-modules. Its
homotopy kernel
is the universal 3-term h-complex in
\mathcal{C}_{dg}(k)
of the form

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{hker}(j)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}

Explicitly, we have
\mathrm{hker}(j)=\Sigma^{-1}\mathrm{Cone}(j)
,
f=[1,0]
, and
h=[1,0]
.

Consider a 3-term h-complex in
\mathcal{A}
as given by (
3.17.1
).
For each object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
, we apply the dg functor
\mathcal{A}(A,-)
to the diagram (
3.17.1
) and obtain a 3-term h-complex in
\mathcal{C}_{dg}(k)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}(A,A_{0})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}(A,A_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}(A,A_{2}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}{(}A{,}f{)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}{(}A{,}h{)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{A}{(}A{,}j{)}}

By the universal property of homotopy kernel, we obtain a canonical morphism

\alpha:\mathcal{A}(A,A_{0})\rightarrow\mathrm{hker}(\mathcal{A}(A,j)).

Dually, we obtain a canonical morphism

\beta:\mathcal{A}(A_{2},A)\rightarrow\mathrm{hker}(\mathcal{A}(f,A)).

Lemma 3.20
.

A 3-term h-complex, as given by (
3.17.1
), is a homotopy left (resp. right) exact sequence if and only if for each object
A
in
\mathcal{A}
, the map
\tau_{\leq 0}(\alpha)
(resp.
\tau_{\leq 0}(\beta)
)is a quasi-isomorphism of complexes of
k
-modules.
It is a homotopy short exact sequence if and only if it is both homotopy left exact and homotopy right exact.

We can restate the lemma in a diagram chasing style as follows: the 3-term h-complex (
3.17.1
) is homotopy left exact if and only if for each
n\leq 0
and each pair of morphisms
(v,w)\in Z^{n}\mathcal{A}(A,A_{1})\times\mathcal{A}^{n-1}(A,A_{2})
such that
d(w)=-j\circ v
, there exists a morphism
u\in Z^{n}\mathcal{A}(A,A_{0})
, unique up to a coboundary, such that there exists a pair of morphisms
(v^{\prime},w^{\prime})\in\mathcal{A}^{n-1}(A,A_{1})\times\mathcal{A}^{n-2}(A,A_{2})
satisfying
v-f\circ u=d(v^{\prime})
,
w-h\circ u=-d(w^{\prime})-j\circ v^{\prime}
.

Next, we reformulate the notions of homotopy (co)cartesian square into the notions of homotopy (pushout) pullback square in
\mathcal{A}
.
Recall that for a dg category
\mathcal{A}
, we denote by
\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{A})
the dg morphism category, cf. Example
2.8
. Objects in
\mathrm{Mor}(\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{A}))
correspond to squares in
\mathcal{A}

(3.20.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b}
\scriptstyle{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}

where
B
,
C
,
B^{\prime}
,
C^{\prime}
are objects in
\mathcal{A}
, and
p
,
c
,
p^{\prime}
and
b
are closed morphisms of degree
0
, and
s
is a morphism of degree
-1
such that
d(s)=cp^{\prime}-pb
.

Definition 3.21
.

The category
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{Sq}}(\mathcal{A})
of homotopy squares in
\mathcal{A}
is defined as

H^{0}(\mathrm{Mor}(\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{A}))).

Lemma 3.22
.

We have a quasi-equivalence

\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Mor}(\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{A})).

In particular, we have an epivalence

\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})\rightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{Mor},H^{0}(\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{A}))).

Proof.

Recall that we have an isomorphism of dg categories
k\mathrm{Sq}\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}k\mathrm{Mor}\otimes k\mathrm{Mor}
and a quasi-equivalence
\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{A})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(k\mathrm{Mor},\mathcal{A})
.
Then for any small dg category
\mathcal{B}
, we have canonical bijections

\displaystyle\mathrm{Hqe}(\mathcal{B},\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A}))

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hqe}(k\mathrm{Sq}\otimes\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hqe}(k\mathrm{Mor}\otimes k\mathrm{Mor}\otimes\mathcal{B},\mathcal{A})

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{Hqe}(\mathcal{B},\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(k\mathrm{Mor},\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{A})).

Therefore we have a quasi-equivalence

\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{rep}_{dg}(k\mathrm{Mor},\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{A})).

Thus, by Lemma
2.11
, we have an epivalence

\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})\xrightarrow{{}_{\sim}}\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Mor},\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{A}))\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Dia}}\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{Mor},H^{0}(\mathrm{Mor}(\mathcal{A}))).

∎

By abuse of terminology, we have the following definition.

Definition 3.23
.

A homotopy square in
\mathcal{A}
, as given by (
3.20.1
), is a homotopy pullback square if the corresponding object in
\mathrm{rep}(k\mathrm{Sq},\mathcal{A})
, under the quasi-equivalence established in Lemma
3.22
, is a homotopy cartesian square, cf. Definition
3.1
. Dually, we define the notion of homotopy pushout square.

We also refer to a homotopy pullback (resp. pushout) square as a
homotopy cartesian
(resp.
cocartesian
)
square
.
A homotopy square that is both homotopy cartesian and homotopy cocartesian will be called
homotopy bicartesian
.
If
Z^{0}(\mathcal{A})
is an additive category, then the object (
3.20.1
) is a homotopy pullback square if and only if the following 3-term homotopy complex in
\mathcal{A}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\oplus C^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}b{,}p^{\prime}{]}^{\intercal}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[}p{,}-c{]}}

is homotopy left exact.

Let us describle
the dg category
\mathrm{Cosp}(\mathcal{A})
of homotopy cospans
in
\mathcal{A}
. It is defined as the dg category
\mathrm{Fun}_{{A}_{\infty}}(k\mathrm{Cosp},\mathcal{A})
of strictly unital
{A}_{\infty}
-functors from
k\mathrm{Cosp}
to
\mathcal{A}
, cf. Subsection
2.3
.
We identify a strictly unital
{A}_{\infty}
-functor
F:k\mathrm{Cosp}\rightarrow\mathcal{A}
with the corresponding diagram in
Z^{0}(\mathcal{A})

(3.23.1)
